If the port\n   111\tdestination is outside the active writable roots, I prepare the result and request the\n   112\truntime's required filesystem approval before writing there.\n     1\t# experimental/ \u2014 experimental brick scope (machine surface)\n     2\t\n     3\tA top-level zsh scope (sibling of `ai/`), promoted out of `ai/experimental/` on\n     4\t2026-09-22: bricks named \"experimental\" but hidden inside `ai/` became orphans.\n     5\tHolds opt-in, portable experiments that earn a run but are not stable enough to\n     6\tbecome a first-class Claude, Codex, or Gemini surface.\n     7\t\n     8\tTwo brick kinds, distinguished by entrypoint filename \u2014 **this is LAW, not\n     9\tconvention** (`base.zsh` PARTITION 3 header repeats it):\n    10\t\n    11\t- `experimental/<id>/runner.zsh` \u2014 **lazy runner.** Nothing loads at shell startup;\n    12\t  the dispatcher only ever executes it on demand via `exp-run <id>`. Mechanical\n    13\t  safety: a broken runner cannot break the shell, only its own invocation.\n    14\t- `experimental/<id>/<id>.zsh` \u2014 **sourced brick.** Loads at shell startup, wired\n    15\t  by explicit name in `base.zsh` PARTITION 3 (never globbed), `zsh -n`-gated (a\n    16\t  syntax error skips the source \u2014 command absent, never shell dead). Must be\n    17\t  define-only: no work, no prints on source.\n    18\t\n    19\t## Layout\n    20\t\n    21\t```text\n    22\texperimental/\n    23\t  base.zsh          signpost \u2014 sourced by config.<machine>.zsh; wires the scope\n    24\t                    + carries the MAINTAINER LOG (per-brick provenance/status)\n    25\t  keyboard.zsh      control panel \u2014 aliases only (exp-list, exp-run, per-brick shims)\n    26\t  dispatcher.zsh    engine \u2014 _exp_list / _exp_run bodies; resolves runners lazily\n    27\t  README.md\n    28\t  <id>/\n    29\t    runner.zsh      lazy runner brick (see Contract) \u2014 OR \u2014\n    30\t    <id>.zsh        sourced brick (see Contract) \u2014 a brick is one or the other,\n    31\t                    never both\n    32\t    registry.json   optional, brick-local convenience data (either kind)\n    33\t```\n    34\t\n    35\t`<id>` is lowercase letters, digits, and hyphens. The dispatcher resolves exactly\n    36\t`experimental/<id>/runner.zsh` for lazy runners; no registry or `deploy.sh` change\n    37\tis needed. A sourced brick instead needs one explicit `base.zsh` PARTITION 3 line\n    38\t(see \"Add a sourced brick\" below) \u2014 `deploy.sh` carries the folder in either way,\n    39\tbut never wires the source line for you.\n    40\t\n    41\t## Contract\n    42\t\n    43\tLazy runners (`<id>/runner.zsh`):\n    44\t- Executable zsh program, not a file sourced at shell startup.\n    45\t- Invoke it with `exp-run <id> [arguments]`; `exp-list` discovers installed runners.\n    46\t- Every runner must support `--help` without making a network request.\n    47\t- A friendly alias may be added to `keyboard.zsh`, but it must delegate to `_exp_run <id>`.\n    48\t\n    49\tSourced bricks (`<id>/<id>.zsh`):\n    50\t- **Define-only.** No work, no prints on source \u2014 functions / `typeset -g` only\n    51\t  (decision 0009 L2 discipline). The command itself does whatever it needs when\n    52\t  the operator invokes it by hand; it must not run itself at source time.\n    53\t- Wired by one **explicit-name** line in `base.zsh` PARTITION 3 \u2014 never a glob.\n    54\t  Add the line in the same edit as the brick's maintainer-log row.\n    55\t- The wiring line is `zsh -n`-gated: a syntax error skips the source instead of\n    56\t  breaking shell startup. This is a mechanical backstop, not a substitute for\n    57\t  testing \u2014 verify with a fresh shell before calling it done (see below).\n    58\t\n    59\tBoth kinds:\n    60\t- Keep API keys, chat transcripts, caches, and provider login state outside this portable\n    61\t  tree. Use the existing machine-local secret surface only at runtime.\n    62\t- Do not give a brick filesystem writes, tool execution, or persistent memory by default.\n    63\t  Add those capabilities only with an explicit task and verification gate.\n    64\t\n    65\t## Maintainer log & approval\n    66\t\n    67\tEvery brick is **EXPERIMENTAL ONLY until @majkee approves** graduation to a first-class\n    68\tsurface. The maintainer log in `base.zsh`'s head records each brick's plug date, origin,\n    69\tand approval status. When you plug a new brick, add its row there. A brick then has two\n    70\tpossible fates: it **graduates** (remove its row, move it out of this scope \u2014 canon\n    71\telsewhere), or it is **trashed** as inappropriate \u2014 see \"Remove a brick\" below; do not\n    72\tjust `rm` the folder and move on.\n    73\t\n    74\t## Add a runner\n    75\t\n    76\t1. Create `experimental/<id>/runner.zsh` in `~/ia-sync/zsh/`.\n    77\t2. Run `zsh -n` on it and `exp-run <id> --help` after deployment.\n    78\t3. Add a row to the maintainer log in `base.zsh` (plug date \u00b7 origin \u00b7 status).\n    79\t4. Add the runner's own README when it has setup, limits, or security notes beyond this\n    80\t   contract.\n    81\t5. Run `bash ~/ia-sync/deploy.sh --dry-run`; recursive zsh deployment carries the folder.\n    82\t\n    83\tThe first runner, `ox-alpha`, is the reference implementation.\n    84\t\n    85\t## Add a sourced brick\n    86\t\n    87\t1. Create `experimental/<id>/<id>.zsh` in `~/ia-sync/zsh/` \u2014 define-only (functions /\n    88\t   `typeset -g`; no work, no prints on source).\n    89\t2. In the SAME edit: add the `base.zsh` PARTITION 3 wiring line (explicit name,\n    90\t   `zsh -n`-gated \u2014 copy the existing pattern) AND the maintainer-log row.\n    91\t3. Run `zsh -n` on the brick, then `bash ~/ia-sync/deploy.sh --dry-run` / `deploy.sh`.\n    92\t4. Verify in a **fresh shell**: the command exists and behaves; then a deliberate-red \u2014\n    93\t   temporarily break the brick's syntax, open a fresh shell, confirm it still boots\n    94\t   clean and the command is simply absent, then restore.\n    95\t\n    96\tThe first sourced brick, `t41` (staged as `4x1`, renamed same-day \u2014 digit-leading names\n    97\tbroke the palette generator's regex; see `base.zsh` maintainer log), is the reference\n    98\timplementation.\n    99\t\n   100\t## Remove a brick (trashed as inappropriate \u2014 not graduated)\n   101\t\n   102\tDifferent fate from graduation above: this is for a brick that earns a **no**, not a\n   103\tcanon promotion. **Two-phase \u2014 quarantine, verify, THEN purge. Never in one step** (a\n   104\tlive shell broken mid-purge is worse than a brick nobody asked to remove yet).\n   105\t\n   106\t1. **Quarantine first.** Cut the brick off without deleting it, so it can no longer be\n   107\t   reached but stays available for one verification cycle:\n   108\t   - Sourced brick: delete (or comment out) its `base.zsh` PARTITION 3 wiring line.\n   109\t   - Lazy runner: nothing to unwire at startup \u2014 it was never sourced. `exp-run <id>`\n   110\t     fails closed the moment the folder is gone in step 3; quarantine is effectively\n   111\t     step 3 for this kind.\n   112\t   - Either kind: remove its row from `keyboard.zsh` if it has a convenience alias\n   113\t     (e.g. an `ox-alpha`-style shim).\n   114\t2. **Verify the quarantine.** `bash ~/ia-sync/deploy.sh` (repo \u2192 this machine), then a\n   115\t   **fresh shell**: the command must be gone, the shell must still boot clean, and no\n   116\t   sibling brick may be affected. Same fresh-shell discipline used to add a brick\n   117\t   (README \"Add a runner\" / \"Add a sourced brick\"), run in reverse.\n   118\t3. **Purge.** Once quarantine is verified: `git rm -r experimental/<id>/` in the repo;\n   119\t   remove its maintainer-log row in `base.zsh` and (sourced bricks) delete the now-empty\n   120\t   PARTITION 3 line entirely, not just comment it; commit.\n   121\t4. **Sweep the live orphan on every host that ever deployed it.** `deploy.sh` has no\n   122\t   `--delete` \u2014 purging the repo folder does NOT remove a machine's live copy:\n   123\t   `rm -rf ~/.config/zsh/experimental/<id>/`. Mirrored by hand on both office and home,\n   124\t   same as any other removal (`SYNC_DISCIPLINE.md`: \"Agents are additive on deploy;\n   125\t   removals are explicit\").\n   126\t5. **Refresh the palette.** `palette-refresh` \u2014 otherwise the trashed brick's commands\n   127\t   linger in `palette.map` as dead entries; the map's staleness check is timestamp-based\n   128\t   (any `.zsh` newer than the map), not content-based, so a pure deletion does not by\n   129\t   itself force a rebuild.\n   130\t6. **Journal it** (`journal.host-cleanup.md`) if the brick ever reached both hosts, so\n   131\t   the other seat knows to run step 4 there too. Check the maintainer log for the\n   132\t   brick's plug history before assuming it never left this machine.\n     1\t#!/usr/bin/env zsh\n     2\t# Experimental dispatcher \u2014 sourced by experimental/base.zsh (P2).\n     3\t# Runners stay lazy: only exp-run sources no experiment at shell startup.\n     4\t#\n     5\t# _exp_list enumerates BOTH brick kinds (2026-09-22 \u2014 was runner-only, a real\n     6\t# discoverability gap: a sourced brick like t41 never showed up). _exp_run stays\n     7\t# runner-only by design (a sourced brick must load in the interactive shell, not\n     8\t# a subprocess) \u2014 it now just says so instead of \"unknown experiment\" when the\n     9\t# id names a real sourced brick.\n    10\t\n    11\ttypeset -g _EXPERIMENTAL_ROOT=\"${_EXPERIMENTAL_ROOT:-${HOME}/.config/zsh/experimental}\"\n    12\t\n    13\t_exp_list() {\n    14\t  local -a dirs\n    15\t  local d id\n    16\t  local -a rows\n    17\t\n    18\t  dirs=( \"${_EXPERIMENTAL_ROOT}\"/*(N/) )   # top-level subdirectories only\n    19\t\n    20\t  for d in \"${dirs[@]}\";
